  • Snapshot source given as a ref (<id>#bracket/face 7) → refused: a snapshot draws a whole document, so its source names no #bracket/face 7: give <id>; --part bracket draws bracket alone (adapt flag name).
  • --json failure → stdout {"ok":false,"error":"…","reason":"invalid_argument"} exactly those three keys, stderr empty, exit 2; without --json message on stderr, stdout empty.
  • Snapshot of an opened STL/GLB/3MF doc draws (parts 1 for STL).
  • (Cheap features) OUT is a folder → <stem>_<YYYYMMDDTHHMMSSZ>.png inside it; trailing separator creates it; a file path with trailing / refused names a folder. Camera "45:30" azimuth:elevation; camera object {position,target,up,zoom}.
  • CORS (security, port first): for Origin in http://127.0.0.1:7878, http://localhost:5173, https://example.com, null, GET /api/docs and /api/health respond with no Access-Control-* headers.

#test_browser → crates/cad/tests/mcp.rs

  • cad_show {source: <doc id>} with a server running answers the doc's /view/<id>?token=… URL and does not start a second server (server.json pid unchanged). Engine down → isError: true with the reason.

#test_codex_contract (+ json) → crates/cad/tests/mcp.rs, crates/cad/tests/fixtures/mcp_contract.json

  • Record our initialize result and tools/list result into a golden JSON, replacing version with <version> and temp paths with <tmp>; test asserts byte-equality after the same substitutions, so any catalog change is deliberate. Separately assert a process that only does initialize + tools/list gets the same catalog.
  • Original shapes to mirror: initialize → {protocolVersion:"2025-06-18" (echo client's), capabilities:{tools:{listChanged:false}, …}, serverInfo:{name:"cad", title:"CAD", version}, instructions}; each tool has inputSchema with "additionalProperties": false, required, property descriptions, and annotations {readOnlyHint, destructiveHint:false, openWorldHint:false}.

#test_server (MCP) → crates/cad/tests/mcp.rs

Drive cad mcp over stdio (newline-delimited JSON-RPC) with a temp HOME and a running server.

  • initialize → serverInfo.name == "cad"; instructions contains the CLI equivalents cad model export <id> <file>, cad model inspect <id>, cad model snapshot <id> out.png, and the words document id and absolute path.
  • tools/list → names exactly our set (cad_run, cad_list, cad_inspect, cad_export, cad_snapshot, cad_interference, cad_show); every schema has additionalProperties:false; readOnlyHint true for list/inspect/interference/show, false for run/export/snapshot. No tool property is named path for the model source — use source (original rule; for us cad_inspect/cad_export/cad_snapshot/cad_show take source = doc id or absolute file path; cad_run takes script).
  • Every property in every schema is accepted by its handler (for each tool, call with required args + each optional arg set to a schema-valid sample; no "unknown argument" error).
  • Tool-level failures are results, not protocol errors: cad_show {source:"parts/bracket.stl"} (relative) → result.isError == true, text contains is not an absolute path; missing file → No file at; a .txt / a .ts script → text contains is not one (not a CAD file); …/x.ts::m → No file at.
  • Bad args message format: cad_show {path:"/abs/x.stl"} → isError, text exactly cad_show: missing source; unknown path. cad_show takes source; source is a document id (running a model's script prints it) or a CAD file's absolute path. (adapt the tail to our wording but keep the missing X; unknown Y. <tool> takes … structure).
  • Unknown tool name → JSON-RPC error -32601 (or -32602), not a crash.
  • cad_show answer text when a browser can't open: begins No browser can be opened from here (<reason>); <name>'s page is http://127.0.0.1:<port>/view/<id> and tells the agent to give the user the URL; when already open: … is already open in your browser at <url>; nothing was opened.
  • File URI decoding for source: file:///home/me/a%20b.step → /home/me/a b.step; on POSIX /C:/x/a.step stays as named. (Windows cases: /C:/Users/me/a.step, file:///c%3A/…, C:\… all → C:\Users\me\a.step; /c/Users/… refused with on Windows an absolute path starts with its drive (C:\Users\...).)
  • initialize and tools/list do not initialise the kernel or contact the server (run with no server and CAD_SERVER_URL pointing at a closed port: both still answer within 1 s).
  • Large results: a cad_snapshot/cad_inspect reply must stay under ~5.6 MB per message (MCP TS SDK stdio reader limit 10 MiB); a too-large image is refused with a short error (< 1 KB) rather than sent.

#test_protocol → crates/cad/tests/mcp.rs (port verbatim; transport-level, independent of tools)

Feed cad mcp stdin lines; read stdout frames (one JSON per line).

  • Slow vs fast: send {"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":1,"method":"tools/call", … slow} then {"id":2,"method":"ping"} → frame for id 2 arrives before id 1. (Need a deliberately slow tool — e.g. cad_run of a script that sleeps — or a test-only method.)
  • Error code preservation: invalid params → {"code":-32602,"message":…,"data":…} kept; an internal panic → -32603 with the message.
  • Notification frames have no id: {"jsonrpc":"2.0","method":"notifications/tools/list_changed"} (no params key when empty).
  • {not json\n → error frame code -32700, then a following ping (id 3) is still answered.
  • notifications/cancelled {requestId:7} for an in-flight request 7 → no response for 7 ever; next request answered; no extra frames.
  • A response object arriving from the host ({"id":"x","result":{}}) is ignored; stream continues.
  • Closing stdin while a call is in flight → process exits 0 within 60 s (doesn't hang on the in-flight call).
  • Stdout is protocol-only: anything else the process or its children print (e.g. cad run output) goes to stderr; stdout contains only JSON frames.
  • ping → {"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":N,"result":{}}.

#test_shim_endpoint → crates/cad/tests/mcp.rs

  • cad mcp re-reads ~/.cad/server.json for every request: restart the server on a new port mid-session → next tool call reaches the new port.
  • Server stopped and a plain TCP listener bound on its old port: the next tool call sends nothing to that listener (assert the listener accepted 0 connections or received 0 bytes — the token must never reach it) and returns one error line.
  • Host closing stdin after the server stopped does not start a server.

#test_shim_serve → crates/cad/tests/mcp.rs

  • Send SIGINT to cad mcp while idle → exits with code 130 (128+2), stderr has no panic/backtrace text. SIGTERM → 143. A second signal during shutdown doesn't change the exit code or produce a backtrace.

#test_shim_start → crates/cad/tests/mcp.rs

  • No server running: cad mcp's initialize is answered within 2 s even while it starts the server (answer has serverInfo {name:"cad", title:"CAD", version}); the server is started exactly once (one server.json, one pid) even with several concurrent tool calls.
  • If the server start fails (e.g. port held, CAD_PORT pointing at it), initialize/tools/list still answer and every tools/call returns one-line error text (isError) instead of hanging.
  • Bad CLI usage cad mcp --bogus → exit 2, unrecognized/unexpected argument on stderr.

#Cheap features the tests exercise that we lack

  • Run-line delta: parts 4 (+2) · bounds … (was …), changed N, materials changed — needs only the previous BuildResult per doc (we already keep version). Also fix singular 1 hit/1 miss in formatSummary.
  • Exact (optimal) bounds in Kernel::bounds (OCCT AddOptimal) — loose boxes over-report lofts/fillets/splines by up to 10%.
  • Interference --fit A B (declared intended overlaps reported as info, exit 0) and a cached result for repeated checks.
  • Mesh instancing in GLB/3MF: identical part geometry (same node hash under different transforms) → one mesh, several nodes/items. We have content hashes already.
  • Deterministic export bytes: fixed STEP header timestamp and fixed zip entry dates for 3MF (reproducible files, no git churn).
  • Opening GLB and 3MF (not only STEP/STL) with cad viewer, plus mesh→mesh conversion without the kernel; refuse Draco and out-of-bounds accessors.
  • Snapshot niceties: OUT as a folder → <stem>_<UTC stamp>.png; --camera az:el; camera object {position,target,up,zoom} (what the viewer's Quick Edit packet carries); --part globs (screw*, comma lists) and "isolate".
  • --json failure envelope {ok:false, error, reason} on stdout for every CLI verb, with reason in a small closed set (invalid_argument, not_running, failed).
  • Stable page token across server restarts (so viewer links survive), cad server start --rotate-token; port roll to the next free port when 7878 is taken, remembered in server.json.
  • Parameter write-back (viewer Save edits the param(...) literal in the script and rebuilds; restore on failure) and parameter preview (rebuild at override values without writing) — we already carry params in the IR; preview is just a build with overridden values under a temporary id.
  • Persistence of documents across server restarts (store last IR + BREP per doc id under ~/.cad/docs/<id>/), so inspect/export/snapshot by id work after a restart without rerunning the script; and "not held → run cad run <script>" message otherwise.
  • cad_view / cad_screenshot MCP tools via the viewer's existing WebSocket (/api/events): server asks the open viewer for its selection refs and a PNG of what the user sees.
  • Streamable HTTP MCP transport on the existing server (POST/DELETE /mcp, Mcp-Session-Id, Origin check → 403) — the original's status-code rules are listed in the test_http row above.

#cad_server: top-level, plugin, viewer HTTP routes, browser e2e

Original root: tests/python/packages/cad_server/ (42 test_*.py + update_handshake.json), plugin/ (3), viewer/ (26 test_*.py + golden/gen_golden.mjs, golden/golden.json), and tests/browser/viewer-e2e.mjs.

Mapping notes for the whole slice:

  • Their server is /api/v1/... with an x-cad-viewer: 1 header guard on writes, a Host gate, and (engine mode) a

    bearer token / signed link / session cookie. Ours (docs/api.md) is /api/... with Authorization: Bearer <token> or ?token= on every /api route except /api/health. Their header guard maps to our token gate; their Host gate (DNS-rebinding defence) has no equivalent yet (see cheap features).

  • Their documents are addressed /<id> with id = file_id(path); ours /view/<id> with UUIDv5 of the absolute

    path (files) or abs script path + "::" + model (models).

  • Their CLI is cad <type> <operation> FILE; ours is cad model <op> ID + cad viewer FILE. Exit-code and

    --json failure contracts port; the command table does not.

#Assertions to port

Shared fixture for server tests (crates/cad/tests/common/mod.rs): start cad server start --foreground --port 0 (or the in-process router) with HOME/state dir pointed at a tempdir; read ~/.cad/server.json for port + token; a tiny HTTP client that can send raw bytes (several tests assert header ABSENCE and keep-alive framing, which a high-level client hides).

#test_cli.py → crates/cad/tests/cli.rs

  • cad (no args) → exit 0, stdout contains Usage: cad (clap's spelling) and lists run, model, viewer, mcp, server.
  • cad -h, cad --help, cad help → exit 0, same usage.
  • cad --version → exit 0, one line starting cad <CARGO_PKG_VERSION>; per docs/cli.md it also names the kernel and kernel version (assert occt appears).
  • cad nope → exit 2, stdout empty, stderr names nope (one error line; clap's unrecognized subcommand 'nope' is fine) and does not dump the full usage. cad stats → at most one suggestion; clap's tip: a similar subcommand exists: 'status' style is acceptable; the suggested command is never run.
  • cad --help / cad nope must not start a server or create ~/.cad/server.json (assert the state dir stays absent; original: help leaves the state folder as it was, for every help spelling at every depth: cad model --help, cad model export --help, cad help model).
  • Trailing args after cad run SCRIPT.ts are forwarded untouched to the script, including --, -x and --port 3245 (original: ["doctor","--port","3245","--","-x"] forwarded verbatim). Test with a script that prints JSON.stringify(process.argv.slice(2)).

#test_cli_contract.py → crates/cad/tests/cli.rs

  • Exit-status classes (keep the original table, adopt for our --json commands): usage, invalid_argument, wrong_type, not_found → 2; not_running, failed, internal_error, engine_unavailable → 1; canceled (Ctrl-C) → 130.
  • Under --json a failure is exactly ONE line on stdout, {"ok":false,"error":"…","reason":"…"}, stderr empty, no backtrace. Cases: cad model inspect --json (missing ID) → 2, reason:"usage"; cad model inspect 00000000-0000-5000-8000-000000000000 --json → not_found, 2; server down for a command that must not autostart → not_running, 1, plus "next":["cad server start"].
  • Without --json the failure text is on stderr, stdout empty: cad model export ID out.xyz → 2, stderr names .xyz and lists the supported suffixes (.step/.stp, .stl, .3mf, .glb, .obj, .dxf, .brep).
  • No abbreviated long options: cad model inspect ID --js → exit 2, stderr suggests --json (clap: tip: a similar argument exists: '--json'). --snapshot --display shadedd → 2 and names shaded as the closest value.
  • Interrupt: SIGINT to cad run of a script that sleeps → exit 130, stderr cad: canceled (or the script's own status if it handles SIGINT; pick one and pin it).
  • A --json crash (panic) is still one JSON document with reason:"internal_error", exit 1.

#test_cli_errors.py → crates/cad/tests/cli.rs

  • A model whose op fails (e.g. fillet radius 50 on a 10 mm box) run via cad run: stderr's FIRST line is error: <message>; next line at <src> where src is src/<file>.ts:<line> of the user's call (docs/cli.md); whole report < 5 lines; exit 1.
  • An error with no message is named by its kind/op (error: fillet failed rather than an empty string).

#test_cli_commands.py → crates/cad/tests/cli.rs

  • Pin our table: cad --help lists exactly server, init, run, model, viewer, mcp (plus clap's help); cad model --help lists exactly list, inspect, export, snapshot, interference. A rename must fail this test.
  • Script where an id is wanted: cad model inspect part.ts → exit 2, stderr part.ts is a model's script, not a document id: \cad run part.ts\ builds its models and prints each one's id (adapted wording; pin whatever we choose). Same for cad model export src/my part.ts out.step (quote the path with a space: `cad run 'src/my part.ts'`).
  • cad viewer part.ts → exit 2, part.ts is a model's script, not a CAD file: \cad run part.ts\ builds its models and prints each one's page.
  • cad viewer notes.txt (exists) → exit 2, reason wrong_type, message contains no CAD file at or is not a STEP/STL file.
  • cad viewer gone.step → exit 2, no such file: gone.step (not_found).
  • cad model snapshot ID out.png --size 10 → 2 (invalid_argument: expects WxH).
  • A path containing # (project#1/bracket#2.step) is accepted whole by cad viewer (our refs use # after the id, never inside a file argument).

#test_cli_reference.py → crates/cad/tests/cli.rs

  • Parse cad --help and cad model --help, cad server --help; every listed command cad <x> / cad model <y> / cad server <z> appears in docs/cli.md's table. (Their generated JSON copy is not ported.)

#test_run.py → crates/cad/tests/cli.rs

  • Project folder with src/model.ts importing ./parts.ts (export const SIZE = 3) and a decoy node_modules/@cad/sdk missing → cad run src/model.ts links the SDK and builds; exit 0; stdout has built src/model.ts::box.
  • A local file named like the SDK (src/sdk.ts or a folder cad/) does not shadow @cad/sdk.
  • Script exit code propagates (script process.exit(3) → cad run exits 3).

#test_output_encoding.py → crates/cad/tests/cli.rs

  • Project folder named Zoë Ω; script prints said: Zoë Ω · × — to stdout and stderr. cad run say.ts with both streams piped: exit 0; stdout decodes strictly as UTF-8 and equals that one line; last stderr line equals it. cad model list output naming that script's path decodes as UTF-8 with the name intact (NFC).

#test_missing_runtime_assets.py → crates/cad/tests/server.rs

  • If the binary was built without the viewer bundle (feature/env that skips embedding): GET / → 404, body The viewer client is not built; build apps/viewer (pnpm -C apps/viewer build) and rebuild cad (pin our wording), while GET /api/health → 200.

#test_installations.py (token only) → crates/cad/tests/state.rs

  • After cad server start, ~/.cad/server.json has mode 0600 (POSIX) and the token is ≥ 32 url-safe chars.
  • A second cad server start while one runs reuses it (same port, pid, token) and does not rewrite the file.
  • server.json readable by others (chmod 0644) → the client refuses to use it: error names the path and not this user's alone (mode 0644); it is never silently replaced.
  • Token with non-token characters ("t"*40 + "\r\nSet-Cookie: x=y") or too short (short) → refused by name (holds no token), never sent in a header.
  • Unreadable file (chmod 0, non-root) → cannot be read (Permission denied, mode 0000); file left as it was.

#test_core_imports.py → boundary check

  • cargo metadata: cad-engine's normal dependencies include cad-kernel and none of cad-kernel-occt, cad-kernel-solidcore, occt-*. packages/sdk has no runtime dependency on three/react (SDK computes no geometry). apps/viewer does not import packages/sdk internals.

#viewer/test_http_layer.py → crates/cad/tests/server.rs + security.rs

Host gate (cheap feature; port if adopted, it is the DNS-rebinding defence that token-in-URL pages still need):

  • Allowed Host values → 200: 127.0.0.1:1, localhost, LOCALHOST, [::1]:3245, [::1], localhost (trimmed). Port is never compared.
  • Host: attacker.example → 403, body exactly {"error":"Host header 'attacker.example' is not a local name; refusing (DNS-rebinding defense)","hint":"open this page at 127.0.0.1 or localhost"}, content-type: application/json; charset=utf-8, cache-control: no-store. Host: EVIL.example:8443 → body names 'evil.example' (lower-cased, port stripped, never the raw header).
  • Gate covers every route incl. /, /assets/*, /view/<id>, /api/docs, unknown /api/nope, and HEAD. Host gate wins over the token gate (bad Host + good token → 403 DNS-rebinding).
  • Unit cases for hostname_only: [::1]:3245→::1; 127.0.0.1:80:80→127.0.0.1:80; 127.0.0.1:abc unchanged; localhost: unchanged; [bad unchanged. host_is_allowed: "" allowed; ::1 not allowed when bound to 127.0.0.1; 127.0.0.1.evil.example refused; 0.0.0.0 refused; binding a non-loopback address disables the gate.

    Write guard → our token gate:

  • POST /api/build with no token → 403 (or 401; pick and pin) with JSON {"error":…,"hint":…}; same for DELETE /api/docs/:id, POST /api/docs/:id/export, /snapshot, /interference, /api/open. Unknown routes are gated too (POST /anything/at/all no token → 403, never 404, so the gate runs before dispatch). Empty Authorization: Bearer is refused. GET /api/health needs no token.

    Health payload (their /api/v1/server):

  • GET /api/health → 200, keys exactly ok, version, kernel, kernelVersion in that order; cache-control: no-store; JSON compact (no ", " or ": " in the body) and not ASCII-escaped. Consider adding pid, port, instance (16 hex, stable for the process) — the viewer reloads when instance changes.
  • Unknown query params ignored (/api/health?file=/anything.step → 200).

    Documents (their envelope → our Doc):

  • POST /api/open {"path": "/abs/part.stl"} (ASCII STL solid p\nendsolid p\n is not a solid; use a one-facet or a real STL fixture) → 200 Doc with id == uuidv5(URL_NS, abs path), file == abs path, script == null; then GET /api/docs/<id> → same doc.
  • POST /api/open with relative path part.stl → 400, error contains absolute path, has hint. Missing file → 404 {error,hint}. .txt → 400 is not one (a STEP/STL). Body with {"file": …} instead of path → 400.
  • GET /api/docs/not-an-id → 404, error == "not-an-id is not a document id". Valid UUID not held → 404 naming the id.
  • Changing the file's bytes and re-opening bumps version.

    Static + SPA:

  • GET / → 200 text/html; charset=utf-8 containing <!doctype html>. GET /view/<anything> → SPA html. GET /assets/<real>.js → 200 text/javascript; charset=utf-8 byte-exact. GET /assets/missing.js → 404 text/plain; charset=utf-8 body Not found (never the SPA html). woff2 → font/woff2, ttf → font/ttf.
  • /assets/%zz.js → 400 Bad request; /assets/%C0%AF.js and /assets/%ED%A0%80.js (overlong / lone surrogate) → 400; /assets/%2e%2e%2f%2e%2e%2fetc%2fpasswd → 403 Forbidden (or 404), never file bytes.
  • Unknown API routes → 404 JSON {error,hint}, never the SPA: /api/nope, /api/v2/x, /api, /api/docs/x/y/z/w. Exact body for /api/nope: {"error":"no route /api/nope","hint":"…"}.
  • GET //evil.example/api/health (authority-form-ish) routes on the path only → 200 JSON.
  • /index.html%00.js → not 500.

    Methods:

  • OPTIONS and TRACE on /api/health → 405, empty body, content-length: 0, no content-type.
  • Wrong method on a known route → 405 with Allow naming the route's methods and JSON {"error":"<M> is not allowed on <path>","hint":"use <methods>"}: GET /api/build (Allow POST), POST /api/health (GET, HEAD), PUT /api/docs (GET, HEAD), GET /api/docs/<id>/export (POST).
  • Unknown POST route with token → 404 JSON, no Allow header.

    Header contract (raw socket):

  • No Server header on any response (/, /api/health, /assets/missing.js, /api/nope, /nope). Date present everywhere.
  • No text/html on any error response (/api/nope, missing asset, OPTIONS /, BOGUS / HTTP/1.1, GET / HTTP/9.9, \r\n\r\n). Unknown method BOGUS → 405 with header set exactly {date, allow, content-length}. GET / HTTP/9.9 → HTTP/1.x status, headers exactly {date, content-length}, connection closed. (hyper/axum may differ: assert "no HTML, no Server header" and loosen the exact header set if needed.)

    Keep-alive framing:

  • POST /api/nope with Content-Length: 5 body HELLO then GET /api/health on the same connection → two HTTP/1.1 responses, second contains health JSON, no HELLOGET echo.
  • HEAD /assets/app.js then GET /api/health on one connection → no JS bytes in the stream, two responses.
  • Two GETs on one connection → both answered.

    Bodies:

  • Content-Length over the cap on POST /api/build (pick a cap, e.g. 64 MiB) → 413 (theirs: 400 request body too large) answered BEFORE close; connection: close. A 1 MiB body to a small-cap route still yields a readable HTTP/1.1 413 (body drained before close).
  • Transfer-Encoding: chunked POST → either decoded correctly (hyper does) or refused 400 naming chunked; never a mangled body. (axum handles chunked; port as "chunked build body parses".)

    Streaming (if we add a files route): file replaced atomically mid-request serves either old or new bytes with an accurate content-length; 3 MiB asset streams with exact length; HEAD has GET's headers and empty body.

#viewer/test_security.py → crates/cad/tests/security.rs

Applies to every route that takes a filesystem path: POST /api/open {path}, POST /api/docs/:id/export {files}, POST /api/docs/:id/snapshot {out}, and any future files route. SECRET = TOP-SECRET-BYTES; every denial asserts status AND that SECRET is absent from the body.

  • Relative paths refused 400: ok.step, ../project/ok.step, \\server\share\x.step, C:\x.step (on POSIX). Empty / missing param → 400.
  • Null byte: path /root/ok.step\0.png → 400, body exactly {"error":"File path contains an invalid null byte","hint":"fix the request as the error says"} (adapt wording, pin it).
  • 5000-char filename /aaaa…a.step → 404 (or 400), never 500.
  • Filename with \r\nX-Evil: 1 → 400/404 and no x-evil response header.
  • /api/open of non-CAD files never returns their bytes or parses them: secrets.json, .env, id_rsa, model.py, loose.js, .dotfile.step (hidden file), part.step.json → 4xx, SECRET absent. /etc/passwd → 4xx. A directory named dir.step → 404.
  • A hidden FOLDER on the way is fine: /tmp/x/.worktree/inside.step opens; dot segments collapse (root + "/.worktree/../ok.step" opens ok.step).
  • 404 names the path: No file at /abs/meshes/upper_arm.stl; wrong extension: starts with /abs/…/upper_arm.dae is not a file the viewer sends (adapt: is not a STEP or STL file).
  • Export/snapshot output paths: relative out.png → 400; outside-writable checks: writing to a path whose parent does not exist → 4xx with the path named; never follows a .. that the client did not literally send (paths are taken as given, normalised, then used).
  • Routing table (port the cases, mapped to /api): /api/../etc/passwd → SPA 200 or 404, never the file; /api/%2e%2e/etc/passwd → same; /api//health → 404; /API/health → SPA (case-sensitive API prefix); backslash /api\health → treat as separator (200) or 404, never a file.
  • Malformed escape ?path=%zz → 400/404 and the server still answers /api/health afterwards.
  • No Access-Control-* header on ANY response: /, assets, missing asset, /api/health, /api/nope, /api/docs, refused-host request, untokened POST, OPTIONS /.
  • Viewer page responses carry content-security-policy: frame-ancestors 'self'. Any raw file served back (if a files route exists) carries x-content-type-options: nosniff and content-security-policy: default-src 'none'; sandbox, and no content-disposition, cache-control: no-store; STEP content-type application/step.

#viewer/test_launcher.py → crates/cad/tests/server.rs, cli.rs

  • Three cad viewer --json launches from different cwds → same pid and port (one server); printed url path is / when no file is given.
  • cad viewer part.stl --json → url path /view/<uuidv5(abs part.stl)>; documents[0].id is that id. A symlink link.stl → part.stl launches to the SAME id (canonical path). GET /api/docs/<id> → 200 with file realpath == part.stl.
  • A second server start on another port while one runs → refused, error contains already serves.
  • Every route needs the token: without it /, /assets/app.js, /view/<id>, /api/docs → 403 (/api/health excepted). With Sec-Fetch-Dest: document and no token, / returns an HTML page that has no <script and no ?token= (a "token needed" page).
  • ?token=<token> on a page navigation → 200 viewer html, referrer-policy: no-referrer, cache-control: no-store (the token is in the URL; it must not leak via Referer). If we add a session cookie: Set-Cookie: cad-…=…; Path=/; Max-Age=2592000; HttpOnly; SameSite=Strict, never containing the token itself.
  • ?token=%C3%A9 (garbage) → 403.
  • Sec-Fetch-Site: same-site / cross-site with only a cookie → 403; same-origin/none → 200; with a Bearer header any site → 200.
  • cad viewer JSON never prints the master token if we introduce a separate link token (theirs: link ≠ master; ours today prints the token in the URL — note in cheap features).

#viewer/test_listener_bounds.py → crates/cad/tests/security.rs

  • Send GET / HTTP/1.1\r\nHost: localhost\r\n (headers never finished): server closes the socket within its read timeout (make it configurable for tests, e.g. 0.5 s; assert closed < 10 s).
  • With max connections set to 2 (test knob), two half-open connections hold slots; a third is closed at accept; after closing the two, /api/health → 200.
  • Requests refused at the Host/token gate do not count as activity (if we add idle shutdown, they must not keep the server alive).

#viewer/test_document_dispatch.py → crates/cad/tests/server.rs

  • POST /api/docs → 405 with Allow; GET /api/docs/extra/more → 404; GET /api/docs/ → 404; GET /api//docs → 404; GET /api/docs → 200; POST /api/docs/<id>/mesh → 405 with Allow: GET, HEAD. Every body parses as JSON.
  • Every JSON response carries x-content-type-options: nosniff. HEAD on a JSON route keeps content-length of the GET.

#viewer/test_scanner.py → crates/cad/tests/server.rs

  • POST /api/open / cad viewer accept .step, .stp, .stl case-insensitively (A.STL, vendor.STP); refuse .sdf .json .js .txt, no extension, .stepx, and .py/.ts scripts (scripts are never executed: a .ts whose top-level throws must not throw on open).
  • Hidden file .hidden.stl refused; file under hidden folder .worktree/part.stl accepted; directory folder.step refused; dangling symlink refused; symlink to a file opens with the same id as the target (canonical path) and file shown as… pin: the canonical path.
  • Doc version changes when the file's bytes change; an unrelated sidecar (part.step.json) written beside it does not change the doc.

#viewer/test_library.py + test_recents.py (adapted) → crates/cad/tests/server.rs

  • GET /api/docs on a fresh server → [].
  • After cad run of model A then opening file B then re-running A: list order is newest first by updatedAt (A, B). Re-opening B without changing it does not bump its updatedAt? (pin: opening is activity → bumps; choose and document).
  • DELETE /api/docs/<file doc id> → {ok:true}; the file on disk still exists; doc gone from list; GET /api/docs/<id> → 404. DELETE an unknown id → 404.
  • A model whose script was deleted is still listed (geometry kept) — pin whether script stays and add scriptPresent: false if adopted.
  • Two concurrent cad runs of different models both appear (no lost update).

#viewer/test_xbf_route.py → crates/cad/tests/server.rs, crates/cad-engine/tests/step_import.rs

  • Fixture STEP: assembly pair of plate = box 10×6×4 minus cylinder r1 h10 (colour sRGB (0.8,0.1,0.1) = #cc1a1a) and peg = cylinder r1 h6 translated (8,0,4), material Al 6061-T6 density 2.70 g/cm³. Generate with our own cad model export from an SDK model (part("plate").color("#cc1a1a"), material({name:"Alu_G6061_T6", density:2.7})), then POST /api/open it.
  • Opened doc's parts paths (sorted) == ["pair/peg","pair/plate"]; plate color == "#cc1a1a" (colour round-trips through STEP); peg material.name == "Alu_G6061_T6", density == 2.7, mass ≈ 2.7 × volume/1000 g (if STEP materials survive; otherwise drop that assertion and record it). Volumes: plate = 240 − π·1²·4 ≈ 227.43 mm³; peg = π·1²·6 ≈ 18.85 mm³.
  • Opening the same path again → same id, same version, and the engine does not re-parse (expose a parse counter in /api/health debug or assert ms fast-path); touching mtime only (same bytes) → no re-parse (content-hash cache), version may bump or not — pin.
  • Doc routes for an opened file require the token like any other.

#viewer/test_browser_open.py → crates/cad/tests/cli.rs (only if cad viewer/cad run opens a browser)

  • The opener receives a file://…/open-<16hex>.html path in the state dir, never the tokened URL; file mode 0600; contents contain url=<tokened url> meta refresh and location.replace("<tokened url>"); removed after a delay.
  • Stale open-*.html older than the delay are removed by the next open.
  • No browser available → error says no browser, no file left. SSH_CONNECTION set with no DISPLAY → remote session, nothing opened, just print the URL; Linux with no DISPLAY/WAYLAND_DISPLAY → no display; macOS always has one.
  • State dir writable by others (0777) → refuse: can be written by others.

#tests/browser/viewer-e2e.mjs → tests/e2e/viewer.spec.ts (Playwright)

Setup: start cad server start --foreground --port 0 in a temp project; fixtures smoke.stl, assembly.step (multi-part), hinge.step plus .revision/hinge.step (grown, max x = 158) and .revision/hinge-first.step (max x = 58). Viewport 1024×640, deviceScaleFactor 1; Chromium with --use-angle=swiftshader --enable-unsafe-swiftshader on Linux. Open each file via POST /api/open → navigate to /view/<id>?token=…. Format gate (per fixture: stl, step):

  • Viewer mounts; canvas visible; load finished (pick a seam: [aria-busy="false"] on the viewport and a test hook window.__cadModelBounds() returning non-empty {min,max}); then two rAFs.
  • No dialog over a freshly opened file.
  • Canvas screenshot foreground coverage ≥ 0.03 (sample every 11th pixel; background = most common colour bucketed to 8; covered if |ΔR|+|ΔG|+|ΔB| > 32).
  • Tool strip contains the expected tools for the format (ours: e.g. Select, Measure, Clip, Quick Edit — pin our labels); a tool that does not apply is hidden, not disabled.
  • Right-click on the canvas at (12%, 86%) opens a role=menu with Zoom to fit (and Zoom to selection if we have it); a multi-part STEP's menu (or part tree) has Expand all / Collapse all if we have a tree.
  • No page errors, no console errors, no API response ≥ 400, and no request to another origin (route-intercept and abort cross-origin requests; record them as failures).
  • Any request with a file path query uses an absolute path.

    Camera gate:

  • Opening a STEP fits the camera once; switching render mode (shaded ↔ wireframe/hidden-line; their Inspect↔Render) and back returns the camera exactly (relative drift ≤ 1e-6 over position/target/zoom/halfHeight).
  • Orbit (drag 180,70 px in 12 steps) + wheel −420 moves the camera; a mode round trip keeps that hand-set camera.
  • Opening a different model frames it against its own bounds (drift vs previous > 1e-3); "Zoom to fit" on a fresh fit doesn't move it.
  • Live revision: rewrite hinge.step on disk (or re-run the model) to the grown version → an UNTOUCHED camera re-fits (halfHeight grows, zoom reads 100%, view direction unchanged ≤ 1e-6). After the user orbits (camera untouched: false), rebuilding back to the small version keeps the camera exactly; pressing f re-fits about the new box centre, same direction, zoom 1, untouched: true again.
  • Needs test hooks: window.__cadCamera() → {projection, position, target, up, zoom, halfHeight, zoomPercent, aspect, settling, untouched}, window.__cadModelBounds().

#Cheap features the tests exercise that we lack

  • Host-header gate (DNS-rebinding defence) on every route, with the exact 403 JSON; ~30 lines of axum middleware. Today a rebinding page can't read without the token, but /api/health and the token-needed page are reachable, and a token in a URL is one leak away.
  • Uniform JSON error shape {error, hint} for 400/403/404/405 with Allow on 405, cache-control: no-store, x-content-type-options: nosniff, no Server header; referrer-policy: no-referrer on pages opened with ?token=.
  • --json on every cad command with the one-document failure contract (ok:false, error, reason) and the 2/1/130 exit classes; agents branch on reason.
  • cad viewer / cad run "script given where a file/id is wanted" refusals that name cad run SCRIPT.ts.
  • Request-size cap (413) and slow-client read timeout + connection cap on the server.
  • Owner-only (0600) ~/.cad/server.json, refusing a world-readable or foreign-owned one.
  • A separate long-lived "link" token for printed URLs (rotatable) distinct from the API token, plus an HttpOnly SameSite=Strict session cookie so the token leaves the address bar after first load.
  • Browser opening via an owner-only redirect file (token never on a process command line); skip opening on SSH/no-display and just print the URL.
  • instance id (16 hex) in /api/health so an open viewer reloads after a server restart.
  • 202 {state:"reading", retryMs, building:{stage,done,total}} + Retry-After for large STEP opens instead of a blocking POST /api/open.
  • Browser download of exports: GET /api/docs/:id/export?format=step streamed with Content-Disposition: attachment; filename="…"; filename*=UTF-8''… (non-ASCII names → _ in the ASCII fallback, e.g. ブラケット v2 → "_____ v2.step").
  • Snapshot that takes a full camera {position, target, up, orthographicHalfHeight?} so a Quick Edit view can be replayed exactly by cad model snapshot.
  • Clear hint when libocct_capi.so cannot be loaded (names the missing file and that it must sit next to the binary).
  • Viewer 2D preview of the DXF we export (their drawing payload: LINE/ARC/CIRCLE/LWPOLYLINE-with-bulge/SPLINE).

#packages/core (viewer core library: refs, prompts, picking, clip, camera, mesh decoding) — 113 files

Context: packages/core is the original viewer's framework-free core (node:test + three in node). Their engine document format is XBF (/api/v1/documents/xbf/<id>/structure JSON + one XBM2 binary buffer per part prototype, instanced by 4×4 matrices). Our equivalent is GET /api/docs/:id (Doc) + GET /api/docs/:id/mesh (base64 MeshPayload, one entry per part, no instancing). So XBF transport/caching tests are not portable, but the behaviour tested over the XBF scene (picking rules, refs, parameters) is, against apps/viewer/src/viewer/CadScene.ts + mesh.ts. Most of the rest is their photographic studio / theme / GLB-animation / KiCad / URDF stack, which we do not have.

#Assertions to port

#cadRefs.test.js → apps/viewer/src/refs.test.ts (+ Rust parity)

Design decision to make first. Their grammar: REF = [FILE '#'] SELECTOR (',' SELECTOR)*, SELECTOR = OWNER | [OWNER '/'] ELEMENT, OWNER = SEGMENT ('/' SEGMENT)*, SEGMENT = LABEL | LABEL '.' N | QUOTED ['.' N], ELEMENT = name | face N | edge N | vertex N. Their element numbers count from 1; docs/api.md says ours are 0-based (face <n> kernel order). Pick one and write it into the fixture. Ours today: <id> / <id>#<part path> / <id>#<part path>/face <n> / .../edge <n>; FILE is our doc id (UUIDv5). Keep one JSON fixture read by both the TS parser and the Rust inspect ref parser so they cannot drift (their exact rationale).

Parse cases to port (adapt FILE to a doc id; drop named elements f:…/e:… since we have none):

  • <id>#hand/finger.2/face 7 → file <id>, owner ["hand","finger.2"], element face 7, printed hand/finger.2/face 7.
  • #hand → file null, owner ["hand"], element null (leading # names no file).
  • face 3 (no owner) → owner null, element face 3 (the document root's element).
  • <id>#hand/finger.2,face 3,edge 4 → a bare element after an owner inherits that owner: selectors hand/finger.2, hand/finger.2/face 3, hand/finger.2/edge 4.
  • hand/finger.2/edge12,vertex1 → space optional: printed hand/finger.2/edge 12, hand/finger.2/vertex 1.
  • kit/v1.5 bracket/face 2 → label read whole (spaces and dots are its own).
  • "M3/M4 screw"/edge 2,"M3, M4"/face 1,"say \"hi\"" → labels with /, , or " are JSON-quoted; comma inside quotes separates nothing; printed identically.
  • kit/"a".2/face 1 and kit/ "a b".2 /face 1 → quoted label + sibling index; blanks around segment allowed, printed kit/"a b".2/face 1.
  • kit/"face 3" → a quoted segment is a label even if it reads as an element; kit/face 03 and kit/Face 3 are labels (no leading zeros, case-sensitive face).
  • <id>#kit/part #2/face 1 → only the first # ends the file; later # belongs to a label. "a#b"/face 1 → no file (quote before #).
  • Whitespace around segments trimmed (" kit /face 3 , edge 2 " → kit/face 3, edge 2).
  • Refused (parse → null): "", " ", <id>#, <id># , kit//face 1, /kit, kit/, kit,, kit/"a (unclosed), kit/"a\x" (bad JSON escape), "a" b/face 1, kit/"a" .2, kit/"a".02.
  • Copy-line token (buildCadRefToken/parseCadRefToken): path containing space or # is JSON-quoted: "/work/my parts/kit.xbf"#…; selectors written in given order, de-duplicated ([a,b,a] → a,b); {cadPath:"plate.stl"} → plate.stl#; {} → #; parse stops at newline (token + "\nmore text" parses to token); unclosed quote → null; plate.stl (no #) → null.
  • Document id recognition: lowercase UUID 6f1c2a4e-1b2c-5d3e-8f4a-0123456789ab is an engine doc; the UPPERCASE form is not.

Canonical spelling (viewer copies, inspect resolves), from spellingCases — port to our PartInfo.path rule:

  • Path = labels from root joined by /; a label that names exactly one occurrence may be written alone; same-label siblings are numbered from 1 (kit/hand/finger.1, .2, .3); a label that collides with an element spelling, contains / , ", or ends in .N is quoted ("face 3", "M3, M4", kit/"a".2, a.2 as literal label stays a.2 only when unambiguous).
  • One-part document: the part is its own label; elements under it plate/face 3, bolt/edge 5.
  • An entry from another tree spells "".

#prompt.test.js → apps/viewer/src/components/quickEdit.test.ts

Their message format (our quickEditPrompt currently writes File: <id>\nReferences:\n- <ref>\nRequest: …; decide whether to adopt theirs):

  • formatPromptMessage(text "Round this edge.\n", file ref, face ref, annotation png) →

    Round this edge.\n\nFile: "/work/STEP/my part.step"\nReferences:\n"/work/STEP/my part.step"#bracket/f:bracket.extrude1.3\nAnnotation: /tmp/part-annotation.png i.e. user text (trimmed) + blank line + File: <quoted-if-needed path> + References: + one full ref line per reference (no - bullet) + <Label>: <saved path> per attachment.

  • An attachment not saved to a file is not named in the text: Why?\n\nFile: "…".
  • formatPromptContextText → <ref line>\n<text>.
  • Reference lines: formatPromptReference({resource, selectors:[s]}) = <path>#<s>; parse back round-trips; refused selectors (kit//face 1, bracket/) throw /invalid CAD/; parsePromptReference('/x#kit/') → null.
  • Code range target: {start:{line:4,character:2}, end:{line:6,character:0}} on /work/src/bracket.py → /work/src/bracket.py:5:3-7:1 (1-based); ids 1:5–3:1 (en dash).
  • Document by id: parsePromptReference("<uuid>#face 1") → {resource:{kind:"cad-model", path:<uuid>}, selectors:["face 1"]}, formats back identically; /m/parts.py::cube#face 1 → null; a cad-model resource with a path → throws /by its id/.
  • Validation: duplicate part ids → /unique/; attachment about a missing id → /absent reference/; unknown target kind → /unknown reference/; non-absolute paths (STEP/relative.step, /work/../outside.step, C:\work\a.step) → /absolute/; attachment path not a string → /attachment path/.
  • Delivery ledger {maxPending:2, maxRemembered:3, busyMessage:'busy'}: same op id returns the same promise; 3rd concurrent → {status:'failed', message:'busy'}; throw/reject → {status:'failed', message:<error>} and is forgotten (retry allowed); completed ops evicted oldest-first past 3.
  • displayPath(p, {start:'/Users/jake/robots/demo', home:'/Users/jake'}): inside start → relative (boards/lfo.kicad_pcb); inside home → ~/robots/other/a.step; /Users/jakeX/a.step unchanged (prefix is not a folder); /tmp/... unchanged; null folders → absolute; start of /, /Users, /Users/jake, '' is no workspace → ~/w/a.step; Windows drive case-insensitive (C:/Users/Jake/w/a.step with start c:/users/jake/w → a.step); an id stays as is. Message uses display paths; the stored reference keeps absolute paths; folders promise rejecting → absolute paths.
  • resolvePromptMessage: inline text attachment appended as \n\nSnapshot JSON:\n{...} without reading bytes; else \n\n<Label>: <path>; inline rejection propagates.

#xbf.test.js (picking subset) → apps/viewer/src/viewer/pick.test.ts, mesh.test.ts

Fixture to rebuild as an SDK model (theirs is bracket): plate (x ∈ [-20,20], top at z=8, through hole r=4 at origin, vertical fillets r=2, a painted top face) + two pegs r=3 standing on it at (±12, 0, 8), 6 tall (top vertex (-9,0,14) for the second peg). Serve via our server, record /api/docs/:id/mesh as a JSON fixture. Camera: orthographic ±30, at (0,0,100) looking −Z, view 600×600 px ⇒ 10 px per mm. Their pick reach constants (XBF_PICK_PIXELS): edge 10, edgeHover 6, edgeTouch 20, edgeTouchOverFace 16, edgeDrawn 2 px.

  • Ray at (−7, 5): face on the plate (part 0), its index is the painted top face.
  • Ray at (12, 2.95) (0.5 px inside a peg's rim): edge of peg instance 1 — the rim is that face's own edge, so it beats the face.
  • Ray at (12, 0): face of peg (its top); that face's edges include the rim just picked.
  • Ray at (12, 3.05) (0.5 px outside the rim, over the plate): face of the plate — an edge wins only over a face it bounds.
  • Ray at (12, 2.35) (6 px inside): edge on press, face on hover (hover reach 6 px < press 10 px).
  • Ray at (−20.3, 5) (3 px off the plate's side, nothing under): silhouette edge of the plate; at (−21, 5) → null.
  • With edgeDrawn (2 px) reach: (−20.4,5) → null (box select may start), (−20.1,5) → edge, (−15,5) → plate.
  • Filter: {edges:false} at (12,3.05) → face; {faces:false} at (−7,5) → null; parts-only → the part id.
  • Hidden part (setHiddenParts(["i1"])) is never picked; isolating another part makes the rest unpickable.
  • Element id round-trip (ours: <part path>/face <n> ref) and selectionBounds finite.
  • Full 2×2×2 filter matrix: peg top → faces ? face : parts ? part : nothing; rim → edges ? edge : faces ? face : parts ? part : nothing.
  • Read errors: a 404 on a doc read means "document no longer held" (ours: GET /api/docs/:id 404 → viewer shows removed/missing state).
  • mesh.test.ts: base64 decode of f32/u32 LE arrays (empty string → length 0); faceRanges/edgeRanges lookup (RangeIndex.find) maps triangle/vertex index → face/edge index, −1 outside; their "a face the mesher left without triangles is reported, not drawn" → a [first, 0] range must not break lookup.

#xbfParameters.test.js → apps/viewer/src/params.test.ts

Our Param = {name, value, min?, max?, step?, unit?, description?} (no kind/int). Port formatting + validation:

  • Parse: entries without a name, with a non-number value, or a duplicate name are dropped (first wins); [] for null/{}/non-array.
  • Clamp to [min,max]; float rounds to 6 decimals (1/3 → 0.333333, 2.3000000000000003 → 2.3); "2.5" → 2.5; null/""/"wide"/NaN/Infinity → null.
  • Step inference: explicit step wins; else range/100…1000 rounded to a power of ten: [0,90] → 0.1, [1,5] → 0.01, [0,1000] → 10, [0,0.004] → 0.00001; no range → null.
  • Format: 2 with step 0.01, unit mm → 2.00 mm; step 0.5 → 2.5 mm; unit deg → 30° (no space); a value finer than its step is never rounded away (2.125 with step 0.5 → 2.125).
  • (When editing lands) changed set = drafts that differ from doc value (after clamping/rounding), excluding in-flight equal sends; agent notes exactly:

    Saved from the viewer since your last edit: wall 2 -> 3 (bracket.py), holes 6 -> 8 (bracket.py); the script holds these values and is built at them: read it again before you edit it, and keep them (successive saves of one param collapse; back to start → empty) and Shown in the viewer, not saved: wall 2 -> 3.5 (bracket.py), holes 6 -> 8 (spec.py); the script still holds the first values; write the new ones only if asked.

#camera.test.js + renderOptions.test.js + displaySettings.test.js → crates/cad-engine/tests/snapshot_camera.rs

Our snapshot camera is iso|front|back|left|right|top|bottom|[x,y,z]; theirs also accepts az:el and an object.

  • Presets are exactly ["back","bottom","front","iso","left","right","top"], directions toward the camera: front (0,−1,0), back (0,1,0), right (1,0,0), left (−1,0,0), top (0,0,1) with up (0,1,0), bottom (0,0,−1) with up (0,1,0), iso (1,−1,0.8); up (0,0,1) otherwise. (Our viewer's VIEW_DIRS already match.)
  • "45:30" (azimuth:elevation, degrees) → direction (cos30·√½, −cos30·√½, 0.5) i.e. azimuth measured from −Y toward +X. 30:20:5, 30:, :20 → "Unknown camera preset". (Cheap feature for --camera.)
  • Unknown/empty preset → error naming it; non-finite or wrong-length vectors, zero up, zoom ≤ 0 → errors naming the field.
  • Omitted fields derive from bounds: top on bounds (0,0,0)-(2,4,6) → target (1,2,3), position (1,2,>3), up (0,1,0).
  • bounds (0,0,0)-(2,4,6) → center (1,2,3), radius √56/2. Ortho fit at 800×600: left/right = ∓halfHeight·(800/600), near 0.01, far ≥ 1000.
  • Display vocabulary: ours shaded|wireframe|hidden-line; every canonical mode round-trips; aliases (render, solid, rendered, edges, shaded-with-edges, x-ray, wire) are refused with "Unknown display mode" (CLI exit ≠ 0, server 400).

#fileFormats.test.js → crates/cad/tests/cli.rs

  • Export suffix → format, case-insensitive: .step/.stp → STEP, .stl, .3mf, .glb, .obj, .dxf, .brep; .STEP/Kit.STP accepted; unknown suffix (.kicad_pro, .yml) → error listing the supported suffixes, nothing written.

#stlMeshData.test.js → crates/cad-engine/tests/import_stl.rs (PORT)

Fixtures generated in-test. Binary facet: normal (0,0,1), corners (0,0,0),(10,0,0),(0,10,0), 2 attr bytes; header count at byte 80 (u32 LE).

  • 12 facets binary → 12 triangles; with 2 trailing bytes → 12; header text starting solid named by its exporter but binary-sized → still binary, 12.
  • ASCII solid part … endsolid part with 12 facets → 12; with leading UTF-8 BOM, or leading blank lines/whitespace ("\n\n ", "\ufeff\r\n\r\n\t ") → 12.
  • Header count too high (13, 600 000 000, 0xFFFFFFFF) over 12 complete facets → 12 (read whole facets present).
  • Truncated by 20 bytes or by 50 bytes → 11.
  • 2000-facet header but file cut at 120 bytes → error exactly: This is not a complete STL file: its header counts 2,000 triangles (100,084 bytes), and the file is 120 bytes. It may be cut short, or be another kind of file named .stl.
  • HTML page (text not starting with solid) → This is not an STL file: it is text that does not start with "solid", and an STL in text form starts with that word. (also for ; comment before solid, uppercase SOLID, UTF-16 with/without BOM, not a model\n). Must not allocate the bogus count first.
  • 0 bytes → This is not an STL file: it is 0 bytes, and a binary STL is at least 84.; 50 bytes → message containing it is 50 bytes.
  • solid empty\nendsolid empty\n → This STL file holds no triangles: it is 27 bytes.
  • Random binary noise (PK header) → This is not a complete STL file: its header counts N triangles ….

#stlBodies.test.js → crates/cad-engine/tests/import_stl.rs

When cad viewer x.stl / POST /api/open makes a Doc from an STL:

  • Binary STL of tetra (4 tris, at x=30, size 5) then cube (12 tris, size 10): parts ordered by size, largest first: Body 1 (12 tris), Body 2 (4 tris) — not file order.
  • One body → one part named after the file (part.stl); ASCII with one named solid → that name (bracket); two named solids sharing corners → two parts lid, base (never welded).
  • More than 2000 disconnected bodies → not split, one part.

#threeMfMeshData.test.js → crates/cad-engine/tests/export_3mf.rs

As round-trip checks on our 3MF export: the model declares unit="millimeter" (a missing unit attribute means millimeter; accepted names: micron, millimeter, centimeter, inch, foot, meter); each part is a build item/object, addressable by build order 1, 2.1 …; part colours survive as material colours; a part without colour carries no generated default colour.

#glbMeshData.test.js → crates/cad-engine/tests/export_glb.rs

Our GLB export must follow glTF: Y-up, metres. A CAD point (x,y,z) mm is written as (x, z, −y)/1000; their reader converts back with (x,y,z) → (x,−z,y)·1000. Assert: triangle (0,0,0),(1,0,0),(0,0,1) mm exports as (0,0,0),(0.001,0,0),(0,0.001,0) or equivalently with a root node carrying that rotation+scale (pick one and test it). Node name = part label; material baseColorFactor = part colour (sRGB→linear as glTF requires); one mesh per part.

#autoZoom.test.js → apps/viewer/src/viewer/fit.test.ts

  • merge bounds of [{(0,0,0),(1,1,1)}, null, {(2,0,0),(3,1,1)}] → {(0,0,0),(3,1,1)}.
  • PerspectiveCamera fov 48, aspect 1, radius √3, padding 1.2 → distance = 1.2·√3 / sin(24°) (±1e-9); aspect 0.5 → strictly larger (narrower half-angle governs).

#clipPlane.test.js + modelRuntime.test.js + sceneClip.test.js + sectionCaps.test.js → apps/viewer/src/viewer/clip.test.ts

Our ClipState is {enabled, axis, offset (0..1 of bounds), flip}; map their amount ↔ our offset.

  • Bounds x ∈ [−10,30], z ∈ [100,200]: plane at x=20 cuts away 0.25 (flip: 0.75); amount→position→amount round-trips for 0, 0.123, 0.5, 1 both sides (1e-12); no explicit position = middle (z=150, amount 0.5); disabled = 0; plane above top cuts nothing (0), below bottom cuts all (1), flipped below bottom 0.
  • Default plane exposes the opposite side: axis x at 5 in bounds [0,10] with model offset (2,0,0) → normal (−1,0,0), constant 7.
  • A plane at a boundary that cuts nothing activates no clipping (no clipping planes set, shader not recompiled): at=max not flipped, at=min flipped → none; at=max flipped, at=min, default → a plane.
  • Changing axis without a coordinate resets the position to the middle; same axis keeps it; flip never moves the plane.
  • Two 10 mm boxes at x∈[0,10] and [20,30]: default plane (x=15) clips both materials, caps none; plane at x=5 caps only box a; disabling removes all planes and caps; a plane outside all parts (x=40) → no planes.
  • Caps: borrow part geometry (never dispose it on clip removal), follow the part's transform, are not pickable (raycast yields nothing), colour = the part's base colour, opaque (opacity 1) even when the part is translucent or highlighted; restoring leaves part material colour/opacity untouched; moving the plane releases caps of parts no longer crossed.
  • Picks behind the clip plane are ignored (our CadScene skips hits with distanceToPoint < 0) — add an explicit test.
  • Scene radius for bounds (−1,−1,0)-(1,1,2) = |(2,2,2)|/2.

#request.test.js + client.test.js → apps/viewer/src/api.test.ts

Mock fetch. Classify failures into {kind, url, method, operation, detail, status?, payload?}:

  • fetch throws TypeError → kind network, detail Failed to fetch.
  • 503 {error:{message:"Worker unavailable"}} → kind http, status 503, detail Worker unavailable, payload kept (our server: {error: "..."} string form too).
  • HTML 404 body → http 404; 200 with {broken → kind response, message matches /unreadable JSON/.
  • AbortError (thrown by fetch or by .json()) is rethrown as-is.
  • GET with timeout → kind timeout; the same timeout never aborts a POST (our builds/exports are POSTs). Idle-based: a body that keeps arriving within the window succeeds; one that stalls 10 s fails with detail /stopped sending its answer for 10 seconds/.
  • "Engine gone": network failure, or 403 whose error says the token is unknown (ours: 401/403 on bad token) → gone; a 403 for another reason is not.
  • Missing doc: GET /api/docs/<id> 404 → a "missing" error carrying the server's message; a doc deleted while on screen (DELETE / removed WS event) leaves the list and re-fetch is "missing".

#Cheap features the tests exercise that we lack

  • Ref grammar hardening: sibling numbering label.N for duplicate labels (docs/api.md only says paths are "unique"), JSON-quoting labels containing / , " or that look like face N, comma lists with owner inheritance (part,face 3,edge 4), vertex N refs; one shared parity fixture for TS + Rust. Also decide 0- vs 1-based element numbers (theirs 1-based).
  • Measure tool in the viewer: pure math already specced by measurement/measureEntities tests (formatMeasurement → "12.35 mm", perpendicular ⟂ 8.00 mm for parallel planes, centre-to-centre C-C 60.00 mm for circular edges/cylinders, angle ∠ 90.0°, ΔX 1.23 ΔY -5.50 ΔZ 0.00, ⌀ 8.00 mm L 25.13 mm, coplanar picks never read 0.00). Our FaceInfo/EdgeInfo already give planes/normals/radii/centres, so no mesh fitting is needed.
  • az:el camera for cad model snapshot --camera 45:30 and the viewer.
  • Edge-vs-face pick priority by pixel reach (edge 10 px on press, 6 px on hover, edge wins only over a face it bounds) and a pick filter (faces/edges/parts).
  • Robust STL import errors (exact messages above) and STL body splitting into parts for cad viewer x.stl.
  • Param formatting with step precision and ° for degrees in the Params panel.
  • WebGL-unavailable alert (summary "WebGL unavailable", recovery mentions chrome://gpu), viewer screenshot-to-clipboard, box select, explode slider — all small, self-contained viewer features with ready-made tests.

#Viewer kit: packages/ui/src/renderers/kit/** (105 files)

Context: the original kit is a large multi-renderer shell (STEP/GLB/board/schematic/DXF, Preview/Render studio, annotation ink, joints). Our apps/viewer is one 3D page (pages/ViewPage.tsx + viewer/CadScene.ts, ~2.4k lines) with parts tree, face/edge picking, selection panel + Quick Edit copy, measure-between-two-picks, clip, edges/wireframe, projection toggle, view presets + gizmo. It has no test runner yet: add vitest, jsdom, @testing-library/react, @testing-library/user-event to apps/viewer devDependencies and a test script. Most original tests are written against pure helper modules; the port strategy is to extract the same pure helpers into apps/viewer/src/viewer/*.ts / apps/viewer/src/lib/*.ts (e.g. fit.ts, measure.ts, pick.ts, quickEdit.ts) and test those, not to reproduce their component tree.

Totals for this slice (105 files): PORT 5, PORT-ADAPTED 48, OUT-OF-SCOPE 27, N/A 25.

#Assertions to port

Conventions below: "box" bounds are {min:[x,y,z], max:[x,y,z]} (our Bounds), Z up, +Y up the screen on pole views. Camera fixtures use three.js PerspectiveCamera(48, aspect, 0.01, 10000) / OrthographicCamera exactly as the originals.

#camera/viewportCameraFit → fit.test.ts (PORT)

Implement fitFrame(THREE, {camera, target, bounds, aspect, padding?, room?, viewDirection?, viewUp?, nearClip?, minRadius?}) returning {position, target, up, direction, distance, halfHeight, zoom} without mutating the camera.

  • Default view direction [2.1, -1.65, 1.08], up [0,0,1].
  • fitPadding(aspect): {x:1.1, y:1.1} for aspect ≤ 1 and NaN; y eases linearly to 1.25 at 16/9 (aspect = 1 + (16/9-1)/2 → y = 1.175); y = 1.25 for any aspect ≥ 16/9 (3.5 → 1.25). Explicit padding: 1.08 applies to both axes.
  • For bounds sizes [200,10,4], [10,12,180], [120,90,0], [20,20,20] offset by [14,-37,53], at aspects 0.4, 1, 16/9, 2.8, persp and ortho: project the 8 corners; max(maxAbsNdcX*1.1, maxAbsNdcY*padY(aspect)) equals 1 within 1e-8, and every projected z ∈ [-1, 1].
  • Tall [0..10, 0..12, 0..180]: NDC height occupancy = 0.8 at 16/9 and 2.8; = 1/1.1 at 0.4 and 1. Rod [10,300,10] at 16/9: across occupancy = 1/1.1.
  • Fit planning does not mutate camera position/up/zoom or controls target; returned zoom === 1.
  • Changing the lens (setFocalLength(50)) before fitting gives a different distance than scaling the old fit by tan ratio (diff > 0.1); fitting again from a moved/zoomed camera gives identical position/target, zoom 1.
  • Resize scale: viewportFitScale(framing, aspect 0.4) > (aspect 2.8) for bounds [-100..100, -5..5, -2..2]; unaffected by later camera moves; ratios invert exactly (1e-12).
  • Near-clip independence: nearClip: 0.01 fit unchanged after camera.near = 1000 and camera moved.
  • Degenerate point bounds [1,2,3]..[1,2,3] with minRadius: 1: all outputs finite, distance > 0, halfHeight > 0.
  • "Original frame" invariant: fitting [-10,-8,-4]..[20,30,12], aspect 1.5, gives identical position/target/halfHeight/distance after the camera is moved to (4000,-5000,1500), zoom 4, near 900, up (1,0,0).
  • Room: fitRoom(100, 800) === 0.875; fitting [0..40,0..30,0..120] with room 0.875 centres the box (NDC y) (1-room) lower than without (±0.02), its NDC height ratio ≈ room (±0.03), top < 1 - 2*(1-room). (Only port room if our toolbar overlays the top; otherwise drop this case.)

#camera/viewportCameraKit → views.test.ts, fit.test.ts (PORT-ADAPTED)

  • viewportFitScale({fov:48, aspect}): equal for aspect 1, 1.4, 2.4 (1e-9) and = 1/sin(24°); aspect 0.5 > aspect 1, ratio > 1.8. Ortho: {orthographic:true, aspect:1120/756} → 1; aspect:355/756 → 756/355; opened×closed ratio = 1 (1e-12), opened > 2. Degenerate {}, aspect 0/NaN, fov 0/NaN → finite > 0.
  • View presets (ours: iso, front, back, left, right, top, bottom + the original's 12 edges and 8 corners if we add a cube): viewBasis(view, [0,0,1]) returns unit direction exactly equal to view.direction/|view.direction|; up = [0,1,0] when |direction.z| === |direction| (top/bottom), else [0,0,1]. Landed camera: |right.z| < 1e-12, up.z > -1e-12; pole views up.y > 1-1e-12. Original face ids/directions: z [0,0,1] "Jump to top view"; zNeg [0,0,-1] bottom; yNeg [0,-1,0] front; y [0,1,0] back; x [1,0,0] right; xNeg [-1,0,0] left.
  • viewBasis(null), direction [0,0,0], up [0,0,0] → null.
  • reframeReason(state) (adopt for live rebuilds): different model key → "model" even when userMovedCamera; nothing framed yet → "model"; partial load framed then modelComplete → "complete" once, then ""; a rebuild (same key, zero-pose bounds grew [0..40,..], shrank [2..5,1..3,0..1] or same) → ""; userMovedCamera + incomplete → "". (Mode cases only if we add a second viewing mode.)
  • Active preset from camera: camera at target+(0,0,10) → "top"; at (10,10,10) offset → a corner id (non-empty); at (10,3,1) → ""; camera at target → "". From front (0,-10,0), +Z points up the screen (orientation.z[1] ≈ 1); from +X side orientation.x[2] ≈ 1.
  • Keyboard: getKeyboardOrbitCommand({key:"ArrowLeft"}) → {direction:"left", keyId:"ArrowLeft"}; ArrowDown → down; w/a/s/d/W/A/S/D → null. Held ArrowUp 120 frames from (0,-10,5): passes z/|p| > 0.99, ends upside down (up.z < -0.5, y > 0), right stays level (|right.z| < 1e-9 every frame). (Only if we adopt over-the-pole orbit.)

#camera/cameraTouch → cameraTouch.test.ts (PORT-ADAPTED)

Fixtures PLATE [-50,-30,0]..[50,30,4], BRACKET [-50,-30,0]..[50,30,60].

  • Fresh runtime is untouched; after any touch kind (wheel, drag, keys, cube, restore…) untouched = false and rebuildRefits(rt, PLATE, BRACKET) === false; after fitCamera untouched again and rebuildRefits true; cameraHands === 2 (touch + fit).
  • Last touch recorded (wheel then drag → "drag"); "resize" is not a touch and isn't counted.
  • Untouched: PLATE→BRACKET and back re-fit; same box → no; max z 4.000001 → no (tolerance); null previous → no; boundsChanged(PLATE, …max y 31) → true; malformed box {min:[0,0],max:[1,1]} → false.

    This is the rule our ViewPage needs for live rebuilds over WebSocket doc events (today setMesh(payload, fit)).

#camera/useViewportCamera → views.test.ts (PORT-ADAPTED)

Bounds [-20,-10,-5]..[20,10,5], camera at (2000,-3000,700), zoom 8, target (400,100,80). For every preset, persp and ortho: after setView(preset) + transition end, target stays (400,100,80), zoom stays 8, distance = hypot(1600,-3100,620) (1e-6), direction·preset ≈ 1 (1e-8). fit() → target = bounds centre (origin here), zoom 1. setView sets userMovedCamera = true; fit() clears it.

#camera/runtimeCamera → transition.test.ts (PORT-ADAPTED)

Target (10,-20,0), distance 150, ortho camera. Step the transition at 1000/60 ms until done (280 ms ease):

  • every frame |eye-target| = 150 (1e-6); look and screen-up change < 40° per frame.
  • Top view (direction (0,0,1), up (0,1,0)) to "turned over" [x,-y,-z] offset: halfway the eye is > 140 along the start's screen-right; screen-up · start-up > 0.999 throughout; end position equals target (1e-6), end right·start right < -0.999.
  • Off-pole starts (x,y) ∈ {(1e-6,-1e-4),(0.02,-0.03),(0.09,-0.07),(-0.09,0.05),(0.3,-0.2)} never jump.
  • Cancel mid-turn: position unchanged, right level (|right.z| < 1e-9), upside-down state kept.

    Our CadScene.flyTo is the target; at minimum port "constant distance" and "no >40° frame jump between bottom and top views".

#camera/lookAt → lookAt.test.ts (PORT-ADAPTED; needs a double-click "look at" — see cheap features)

iso camera direction [-0.6,-0.6,0.53]; round to 1e-3.

  • Plane, normal [0,0,-1], points rectangle [-60..60]×[-40..40] at z 8 → direction [0,0,1] (toward camera), point [0,0,8], fit {min:[-60,-40,8],max:[60,40,8]}; normal [0.001,-1,0.002] → [0,-1,0] (snap near-axis).
  • Plane with origin [-60,-40,8] and points lifted to z 8.05 → point [0,0,8].
  • Cylinder axis [0,0,1], origin [20,10,0], r 5, rings at z 8 and 20 → direction [-√½,-√½,0], point [20-5√½, 10-5√½, 14]; viewed from [0,0,1] → direction.z = 0.
  • Circle axis [0,0,-1] centre [20,10,20] → direction [0,0,1], point centre.
  • Line along X, points [-60,-40,8]→[60,-40,8] → direction [0, -0.6/h, 0.53/h] with h = hypot(0.6,0.53), point [0,-40,8]; viewed along X still ⟂.
  • Free points (dome) from [0,0,1] → {direction:[0,0,1], point:[0,0,30], fit:null}; from [0,-1,0] → point [0,-10,20].
  • planeOfPoints(square z 8, 0.01).normal abs → [0,0,1]; collinear → null; non-flat → null. boundsAbout([0,0,0], {min:[-1,2,0],max:[3,4,1]}) → {min:[-3,-4,-1],max:[3,4,1]}.

    For us the inputs come from Inspect.face (FaceInfo surface type, normal, center, bounds) / Inspect.edge (curve type, circle axis/centre), not mesh points.

#camera/turntable + orbitControlsPointers → orbit.test.ts (PORT-ADAPTED, only if we keep OrbitControls)

Canvas 400×400 (define clientWidth/Height), setPointerCapture stubbed, errors recorded via window error listener must be [].

  • Pen lands after a touch, touch lifts: no throw, controls.state === -1; subsequent mouse press → state 0, release → -1.
  • Mouse orbiting, a touch pointerup id 7 elsewhere: still state 0; moving changes camera > 0.01.
  • Two touches → state 5 (TOUCH_DOLLY_PAN); lift one → 3 (TOUCH_ROTATE), moving rotates.
  • Turntable (if adopted): vertical drag 150 px from eye (0,-10,5) passes over the top (back.z > 0.99), |right.z| < 1e-9, distance constant (1e-9), ends back.y > 0.5, up.z < -0.5; top view stays exactly top (hypot(x,y) < 1e-12, up.y ≈ 1).

#camera/wheelZoom + zoomPivotReanchor → zoom.test.ts

Constants: mouse zoom speed 5.0, trackpad 7, WHEEL_PINCH_DELTA_BOOST = 10, trackpad cutoff |deltaY| < 20, DEFAULT_ZOOM_SPEED = 4.5, COARSE_POINTER_ZOOM_SPEED = 1.6. Step = 0.95 ** (speed * |normalizedDelta| * 0.01), deltaMode 1 ×16, 2 ×100.

  • {deltaY:100} → 22.6 % per notch; ≥ 9 notches to go 100 → 10; Firefox {deltaY:3, deltaMode:1} between 5 % and 22.6 %.
  • deltas 19 vs 21 rate ratio < 1.5; ctrl-pinch {deltaY:4, ctrlKey} equals plain {deltaY:4} step.
  • isPinchWheelEvent({ctrlKey:true,deltaY:4}) true; isTrackpadLikeWheelEvent({deltaY:100}) false, ({deltaY:3,deltaMode:1}) false, ({deltaY:4}) true.
  • Zoom pivot: camera (0,0,20) looking at origin, unit box at z 5 (+0.5): pointer centre → target [0,0,5.5] (one raycast); pointer miss → [0,0,6] (bounds [0,0,0]..[0,0,2] offset); null/NaN bounds keep target depth; maxDistance 5 → [0,0,15]; minDistance 2 with target 30 → [0,0,18]; ortho unchanged. Pan scale = 14.5/20 over the face, 1 on a miss / ortho.

    Port only if we implement zoom-to-cursor (cheap) — otherwise drop the pivot part.

#inspector/numberText → lib/numberText.test.ts (PORT)

  • splitValueText("7.3 mm") → {number:"7.3", space:" ", unit:"mm"}; "45°" → {"45","","°"}; "-1.5 EV" → {"-1.5"," ","EV"}; "5" → {"5","",""}.
  • readTyped("8.25","mm") → {kind:"number", value:8.25}; " 8 MM " → 8; "1in" → 25.4; "2 cm" → 20; readTyped("1 ft","in") → 12; "1 rad" in "°" → 57.29578 (1e-4); "5 kg" in mm → {kind:"unit", unit:"kg"}; "5 mm" in "" or "%" → {kind:"unit", unit:"mm"}; "abc"/"" → {kind:"invalid"}. isLengthUnit("mm") true, ("°") false.
  • decimalsOf("0.05")=2, ("7")=0, (1e-7)=7; formatNumber(9,1)="9.0", (-0.04,1)="0.0", (127.00001,0)="127"; clampNumber(50,3,9)=9, (-5,3,undefined)=3, (5)=5.

    Use for the parameter editor and any typed numeric field (clip offset, measure).

#inspector/SliderField + FileSheet → SliderField.test.tsx (PORT-ADAPTED; needs a params panel — Doc.params exists, a set-param route does not)

Field: label "hole_d", value 7.3, min 3, max 9, step 0.1, text "7.3 mm".

  • DOM order: label, textbox value "7.3", unit span "mm", slider aria-valuenow="7.3".
  • First press selects all (selection 0..3), second places caret.
  • Type 8.25 + Enter → commit "8.25 mm"; type 4 + blur → "4 mm"; type 5 + Escape → no commit, onValueChange calls [[5],[7.3]], input back to "7.3"; focus+blur unchanged → no commit. Rounded display (123.4 shown "123°") focused/blurred or retyped to the same → no commit.
  • ArrowUp → 7.4; Shift+ArrowDown → 6.4; Shift+Up ×2 → 8.4; Shift+Up → 9.0 (clamped); Enter → "9.0 mm".
  • "1 in" (max 40) → live 25.4, Enter "25.4 mm"; "5 kg" → data-invalid, hint "Unknown unit: kg", Enter reverts to 7.3.
  • "50" → hint "Max 9 mm" (kind range, not invalid), live 9, Enter "9.0 mm"; "1" → "Min 3 mm"; "abc" → invalid, aria-invalid="true", hint "Enter a number, 3–9 mm", blur reverts.
  • Value input (FileSheet): Enter commits exactly once even without blur; Escape discards.

#tools/measure/measureRulerState → measure.test.ts (PORT)

Pure reducer; FIRST={point:[0,0,0]}, SECOND={point:[3,4,0]}, ids m-1…, MAX = 20.

  • pick(null, FIRST) → {draft:{anchor:FIRST, hover:null}, hover:FIRST, measurements:[]}.
  • second pick → draft null, one measurement id m-1, pickA/pickB, euclidean 5.
  • 4 picks → 2 measurements m-1, m-2. Cap: 23 pairs → length 20, first id m-4, last m-23.
  • Invalid picks (null, {point:null}, NaN) from null → null; while drafting → same state object.
  • Held face {referenceId:"top", snapKind:"face"}: pick null → draft cleared, measurements kept; same face elsewhere → draft cleared; with nothing held pick null → same object. Two free picks at the same point → measures (length 1).
  • Hover: while drafting stores draft.hover; 2-D point [2,0] → hover null; without draft and committed → same object; hover identity: same referenceId/snapKind → same object; different → new; hover(null,null) → null.
  • draftMeasurement(hovered (3,4,0)) → euclidean 5; no draft or no hover → null.
  • Delete by id keeps others, draft null; delete on a draft-only state → same object; unknown id → same object.
  • stateForChange(draft) → same; {toolActive:false} → draft null, measurements kept; {entryChanged:true} → null (also from null).
  • cancelDraft drops only draft; no draft → same object; null → null.
  • Colour index from a counter survives deletes: [0,1] → delete first → add → [1,2].
  • Draft hover sliding along the same edge → same object; to a face → draft.hover.referenceId "f2"; off model → hover null; free point is a hover.

    Our ViewPage today measures only between the 2 selected items; port this reducer as measure.ts to support a dedicated Measure tool (M).

#Cheap features the tests exercise that we lack

(Checked against apps/viewer/src: ViewPage, CadScene, SelectionPanel, PartsTree.)

  • Viewer test harness: vitest + jsdom + testing-library in apps/viewer; Playwright for tests/e2e. Nothing exists yet.
  • Untouched-camera rebuild rule (cameraTouch/reframeReason): re-fit on a live rebuild only if the user hasn't moved the camera and the bounds changed. ~30 lines; matters for the cad run → live viewer loop.
  • Persisted per-doc view (camera, display, clip, tree expansion) in localStorage keyed by doc id.
  • Dedicated Measure tool with vertex snapping to edge ends, multiple kept measurements (cap 20), delete/clear, Escape cancels draft — reducer is ~100 lines and fully specified above. Today we only measure between two selected items.
  • Double-click "look at" a face/edge (square the view to a plane, cylinder, circle, line) using Inspect.face/edge data.
  • Zoom to cursor (pivot re-anchor on the surface under the pointer) and zoom to selection (⇧2) / zoom 100 % (⇧0) keyboard shortcuts; F to fit, M for measure.
  • Context menu on the canvas: Copy reference, Select/Deselect, Zoom to fit, Zoom to selection.
  • Hover tooltip naming what a click would pick (part · face n) after 450 ms.
  • Parts-tree filter box (subsequence search with path prefixes, pasted ref finds its row) and auto-expand single-child chains.
  • ⌘C / Ctrl+C copies selected references when focus is in the viewer.
  • Alert card for Doc.error with Retry, collapsible Details, and "Copy error details"; distinguish "couldn't update" (previous geometry kept) from "couldn't build".
  • Params panel with typed-unit numeric fields (in/cm/ft → mm), arrow-key stepping, range hints — needs a server route to set a param and rebuild (Doc.params exists, no setter in docs/api.md).
  • Camera in the Quick Edit prompt (promptCamera() → a line the agent can feed to cad model snapshot --camera).
  • window.__cadViewerLoad load-timeline hook so e2e tests wait on real geometry instead of sleeps.
  • View cube with edge and corner snaps (20 extra presets) and drag-to-orbit; ours has 7 named views + a gizmo.
  • "Report Issue" link prefilled from an alert, with home-path scrubbing and URL length cap (only if the project wants it).

#packages/ui (everything except src/renderers/kit) — 89 files

Context for this slice: the original packages/ui is a multi-renderer file viewer (DXF, KiCad board/schematic, GLB, STL/3MF, robot/URDF, and the "engine document" renderer xbf). xbf is their engine-document format (a structure.json of prototypes/instances/tree + per-part .xbm meshes served by the engine); it is the exact counterpart of our Doc + base64 MeshPayload (docs/api.md), so the renderers/xbf/* tests map onto our apps/viewer/src/pages/ViewPage.tsx, components/PartsTree.tsx, components/SelectionPanel.tsx, viewer/CadScene.ts. Their "catalog / library / tab-store / explorer / Open in / export toasts / i18n / What's new" shell has no counterpart in our viewer (DocList + ViewPage) and is OUT-OF-SCOPE (feature absent) unless the lead adopts one of the cheap features at the end.

Test runner assumption for targets: vitest + @testing-library/react + jsdom for apps/viewer/src/**/*.test.ts(x); Playwright for tests/e2e/*.spec.ts against cad server start --foreground with a built viewer.

#Assertions to port

#cad-viewer/catalog.test.ts → apps/viewer/src/format.test.ts, api.test.ts

Our format.ts has basename; add normalizePath if the viewer ever shows file paths (DocList shows script/file).

  • normalizePath: ['C:\\models\\a.step','/models/parts/','/','C:\\',''] → ['C:/models/a.step','/models/parts','/','C:/',''].
  • basename: '/models/parts/a.step'→'a.step', 'C:\\models\\a.step'→'a.step'; original returns '/' for '/' (ours returns '/' via || p fallback — keep).
  • joinPath('/', 'models')→'/models', joinPath('C:/','models')→'C:/models', joinPath('/models/','a.step')→'/models/a.step' (only if added).
  • Copy link (adapted): copying a document's link yields ${origin}/view/${id}?token=${token} (ours: Doc.url). Assert the copy button on ViewPage writes exactly doc.url (or location.origin + '/view/' + id + '?token=' + getToken()), and that copy-part-ref writes ${id}#${path} (partRef).
  • Copy path of a Windows path is normalised to forward slashes ('C:\\models\\a.step' → 'C:/models/a.step') — apply to "copy script path" if offered.

#cad-viewer/MissingCadFile.test.tsx + CadViewer.test.tsx (failure pages) → ViewPage.test.tsx

Mock api (GET /api/docs/:id → 404 ApiError):

  • Unknown id: page shows a "not found" title (original: File does not exist) and the requested id/path; a "Go home" button navigates to /.
  • Any other error (500, message The viewer stopped): title Could not open that file (ours: pick one wording) plus the server message verbatim, plus Go home.
  • Auto-recover: while showing not-found, a WS {type:"doc", id} event for that id (or a successful refetch) loads the doc once; a second event with the same version does not refetch the mesh again; a new version does (original: same hash → 1 reload, new hash → 2 reloads).
  • Refetch on return: window focus and document visibilitychange each trigger a doc-list refresh (original: refresh called 2× for one focus + one visibilitychange). Ours: DocList refetches /api/docs on both.
  • Build error (adapted from "unsaved/detached" pages): a Doc with error: {message, op, src} and last good geometry shows the error banner with message and at <src> while the mesh stays on screen.

#file-viewer/FileViewer.test.tsx (+ FileViewerGenerations, useFileDocument) → ViewPage.test.tsx

  • Navbar title is the model name (Doc.model), never the UUID: while loading show nothing (or a name passed in), after load show doc.model (original: '' → 'qdd_actuator' → 'qdd actuator').
  • Failure object: loader rejects with ApiError(404) → missing: true; any other error → missing: false; both carry message and a reload() that re-requests the same id (stat called twice with the same path after reload).
  • Stale-load isolation (Generations): navigate /view/A → /view/B while A's /mesh is still pending; resolve A's mesh afterwards → scene/state must still show B (A's late response is dropped). Likewise a late inspect for a previous version must not populate the current selection (our requested key already includes version|ref — assert it).
  • Reload (useFileDocument): a doc event with a higher version refetches /api/docs/:id and /mesh exactly once each and keeps the camera (no fit), first load fits.
  • Keyboard (takeKeyboard + FileViewer): on open, the view container receives focus if document.activeElement === document.body and window.top === window; if an <input> has focus it keeps it; inside an iframe (window.top !== window, or accessing top throws) it does not take focus. Our key handler already ignores INPUT/TEXTAREA targets — assert pressing f inside the request textarea does not call fit, w toggles wireframe, Escape clears selection.

#library/ModelLibrary.test.tsx → DocList.test.tsx, format.test.ts

Original age rules (ageLabel(seconds, now)): 20 s → now, 5 min → 5m ago, 2 h → 2h ago, 3 d → 3d ago, 40 d → toLocaleDateString(undefined,{month:'short',day:'numeric'}), null → ''. Our ago() differs (just now <5 s, Ns ago, then m/h, then a date with year at ≥24 h). Decide one; port the table with our chosen strings:

  • ago(now-2s) = just now; ago(now-20s) = 20s ago; ago(now-300s) = 5m ago; ago(now-7200s) = 2h ago; ago(now-3d) = original 3d ago (ours currently a date — either add a d step or assert date). Non-ISO input returns the input unchanged.
  • Size label (if a file's size is shown): 1_700_000 → 1.6 MB, 900 → 900 B, status 1.6 MB · Viewed 5h ago.
  • Heading count: with 3 docs the heading reads Recent + count 3; typing plate into the search box (searchbox named Search models) leaves count 1. Ours: DocList filter (if added) by model/script name.
  • Delete: a row's Delete asks for confirmation whose text says the source file stays on disk; confirming calls DELETE /api/docs/:id and the row disappears immediately (optimistic); cancelling calls nothing.
  • Each model of one script is its own row (two model() exports in one script → two docs, two rows; nothing folded).
  • Polling: original re-reads every ~2 s while home is up and keeps cards when unchanged; ours uses WS doc/removed events → assert a removed event drops the row and a doc event for a new id adds it without a full reload.
